Flu 突变 Explorer:用于绘制 A 型流感病毒 宿主 适应 突变 的交互式平台

Flu Mutation Explorer: an Interactive Platform for Mapping Host Adaptation Mutations in Influenza A Viruses
作者： Mojsiejczuk, L., Wright, D., Gifford, R. J., Peacock, T. P., Robertson, D. L., Hughes, J. L., Goldhill, D. H., Hutchinson, E.
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在解决解释甲型流感病毒中病毒 突变 生物学意义的挑战,特别是那些与 宿主 适应相关的病毒。基因组 测序的快速扩展需要更好的工具来理解这些 基因组。
设计与对象
该研究描述了名为 Flu 突变 Explorer 的交互式 Web 应用程序的开发,该应用程序的创建是为了支持 突变 注释和系统发育分析。它是为没有接受过生物信息学培训的用户设计的。
核心方法
Flu 突变 Explorer 将大规模流感系统发育与已报告的哺乳动物适应 突变 的手动管理数据库集成在一起。它允许用户查询蛋白质序列、可视化病毒谱系中的氨基酸分布、检查 宿主 特定的保守模式,并识别适应 突变 以及支持文献的链接。
主要结果
The platform includes a database with over 1.5 million publicly available influenza A virus sequences and over 1000 mutations associated with mammalian adaptation. Case studies demonstrate its use in assessing amino acid conservation and identifying candidate mammalian adaptation mutations during the ongoing H5N1 panzootic.
解释与创新
Flu 突变 Explorer 通过将基因组、系统发育和功能信息集成到直观的界面中,提供了解释流感序列的新颖框架。这使得专家和非专家都能够探索遗传变异及其对 宿主 适应的影响。
科研与公卫意义
该平台可以通过快速识别候选哺乳动物适应突变来为监测和风险评估提供信息。它支持了解病毒 进化 及其对公共卫生的潜在影响。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于描述 Flu 突变 Explorer 的开发和功能,没有报告统计分析或实验验证。证据是一般性的,不包括与工具性能相关的具体结果或限制。

Flu Mutation Explorer: an Interactive Platform for Mapping Host Adaptation Mutations in Influenza A Viruses

Authors: Mojsiejczuk, L., Wright, D., Gifford, R. J., Peacock, T. P., Robertson, D. L., Hughes, J. L., Goldhill, D. H., Hutchinson, E.
Original abstract (English)
A rapid expansion of influenza A virus (IAV) genome sequencing has transformed global surveillance but has also created major challenges for interpreting the biological significance of viral mutations, particularly amino acid replacements associated with host adaptation. Resources have been created to support mutation annotation and phylogenetic analysis, but there is a need for a tool that integrates experimentally derived phenotypic evidence with evolutionary context in a framework suitable for users without prior training in bioinformatics. Here, we present the Flu Mutation Explorer, an interactive web application that combines large-scale influenza phylogenies with a manually curated database of reported mammalian adaptation mutations, to enable the exploration and interpretation of IAV genetic variation. The underlying database comprises over 1.5 million publicly available IAV sequences and over 1000 mutations associated with mammalian adaptation. The Flu Mutation Explorer enables users to query protein sequences, visualise amino acid distributions across viral lineages, examine host-specific conservation patterns, and identify adaptation mutation with links to supporting literature. We include case studies which demonstrate the platform's use in assessing amino acid conservation at sites of interest and in rapidly identifying candidate mammalian adaptation mutations during the ongoing H5N1 panzootic. By integrating genomic, phylogenetic, and functional information into an intuitive interface, the Flu Mutation Explorer lowers the barriers to interpreting influenza sequences for specialists and non-specialists alike.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the challenges of interpreting the biological significance of viral mutations in influenza A viruses, particularly those associated with host adaptation. The rapid expansion of genome sequencing has created a need for better tools to understand these mutations.
Design and population
The study describes the development of an interactive web application called the Flu Mutation Explorer, which was created to support mutation annotation and phylogenetic analysis. It is designed for users without prior bioinformatics training.
Core methods
The Flu Mutation Explorer integrates large-scale influenza phylogenies with a manually curated database of reported mammalian adaptation mutations. It allows users to query protein sequences, visualise amino acid distributions across viral lineages, examine host-specific conservation patterns, and identify adaptation mutations with links to supporting literature.
Main results
The platform includes a database with over 1.5 million publicly available influenza A virus sequences and over 1000 mutations associated with mammalian adaptation. Case studies demonstrate its use in assessing amino acid conservation and identifying candidate mammalian adaptation mutations during the ongoing H5N1 panzootic.
Interpretation and novelty
The Flu Mutation Explorer provides a novel framework for interpreting influenza sequences by integrating genomic, phylogenetic, and functional information into an intuitive interface. This enables both specialists and non-specialists to explore genetic variation and its implications for host adaptation.
Scientific and public-health significance
The platform can inform surveillance and risk assessment by enabling rapid identification of candidate mammalian adaptation mutations. It supports the understanding of viral evolution and its potential impact on public health.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to describing the development and features of the Flu Mutation Explorer, with no reported statistical analyses or experimental validation. The evidence is general and does not include specific results or limitations related to the tool's performance.

应对流行病准备的数字健康:对传染病创新、公平差距和治理的批判性回顾 监测

Digital Health for Pandemic Preparedness: A Critical Review of Innovations, Equity Gaps, and Governance in Infectious Disease Surveillance
作者： Abdolreza Babamahmoodi, F. Babamahmoodi, Majid Marjani
摘要中文翻译背景:最近的传染病紧急事件,包括SARS-CoV-2、猴痘(2022-2024年)、美国乳牛中的高致病性禽流感 A(H5N1)(2024-2025年)以及卢旺达的马尔堡病毒(2024年),已经使传统的监测系统不堪重负。数字健康已被提议作为一种范式转变;然而,大多数评论都集中在诊断准确性上,而忽视了公平性和治理。证据获取:我们进行了一项结构化的主题叙事综述,纳入了从2010年1月至2025年3月发表的同行评议文献和灰色文献,并按照PRISMA 2020和PRISMA-ScR进行报告。于2025年2月5日至18日检索了PubMed、Scopus和Web of Science。检索关键词结合了三个概念块:(1)数字健康技术(人工智能、机器学习、移动健康、电子健康、远程医疗、电子健康记录、可穿戴设备、废水监测、基因组监测、自然语言处理);(2)传染病(暴发、大流行、流行病、COVID-19、埃博拉、寨卡、猴痘、流感、抗菌素耐药性、马尔堡);(3)公共卫生功能(监测、检测、预测、接触者追踪、治理、公平性)。提供了完整的布尔语法。在EndNote X20中去重,并由2名盲法审稿人在Rayyan中进行筛选(Cohen κ = 0.81)。使用混合方法评估工具2018评估实证研究,使用Joanna Briggs Institute清单评估综述。综合分析遵循Braun和Clarke的反身主题分析,并通过社会技术系统和数据正义视角进行解释。结果:在1498条记录中,142项研究符合纳入标准;48%(69/142)被评为高质量。证据基础在区域分布上严重偏向(高收入国家[HICs],41%;撒哈拉以南非洲或南亚,19%)。没有技术具有普遍优越性。人工智能(AI)研究集中在高收入国家(32项中的18项,56%),而短信服务(SMS)移动健康研究集中在低收入和中等收入国家(LMICs;25项中的15项,60%)。反复出现的障碍包括算法偏见、碎片化的互操作性、依赖捐赠者的可持续性,以及与低收入和中等收入国家分享病原体数据的缓慢收益共享。结论:在142项纳入研究的综合分析支持下,我们得出一个谨慎的结论:数字健康对于大流行准备是必要但不充分的。证据中最一致观察到的模式——区域偏向、反复出现的治理失败、依赖捐赠者的可持续性,以及决定公平性结果的设计选择——共同表明,公平性、可持续性和问责制是有效性的组成部分,而非障碍。日益受到限制的是政治和制度意愿,而非分析不确定性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述审查了用于大流行防范的数字健康创新,重点关注传染病 监测,核心问题是这些技术如何在包括 禽流感 A(H5N1) 在内的近期大流行背景下解决公平差距和治理挑战。
证据基础与方法
继 PRISMA 2020 和 PRISMA-ScR 之后,对同行评审和灰色文献(2010 年 1 月至 2025 年 3 月)进行结构化主题叙述性回顾,检索 PubMed、Scopus 和 Web of Science(2025 年 2 月 5 日至 18 日)。包括 142 项研究(48% 高质量),使用 2018 年混合方法评估工具和乔安娜·布里格斯研究所清单进行评估,并通过 Braun 和 Clarke 的反思性主题分析进行综合。
共识与结论
数字健康对于大流行病防范来说是必要的,但还不够。区域偏差(41%的高收入国家,19%的撒哈拉以南非洲/南亚)、治理失败、依赖捐助者的可持续性以及不公平的设计选择是一贯的模式。公平、可持续性和问责制是有效性不可或缺的组成部分,政治意愿是具有约束力的约束。
争议与缺口
没有一种技术是普遍优越的;人工智能研究集中在高收入国家 (56%),而 SMS 移动医疗在中低收入国家 (60%) 中占主导地位。反复出现的障碍包括算法偏差、互操作性分散、与中低收入国家的病原体数据利益共享缓慢以及区域代表性不足。
科研与实践启示
优先解决区域不平等问题,提高互操作性,确保可持续资金超越捐助者依赖,并加强与中低收入国家的数据共享机制。加强治理框架,将公平和问责纳入大流行病防范的数字卫生设计中。

Digital Health for Pandemic Preparedness: A Critical Review of Innovations, Equity Gaps, and Governance in Infectious Disease Surveillance

Authors: Abdolreza Babamahmoodi, F. Babamahmoodi, Majid Marjani
Original abstract (English)
Context: Recent infectious disease emergencies, including SARS-CoV-2, mpox (2022 - 2024), highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) in US dairy cattle (2024 - 2025), and the Marburg virus in Rwanda (2024), have strained traditional surveillance systems. Digital health has been proposed as a paradigm shift; however, most reviews have focused on diagnostic accuracy while neglecting equity and governance. Evidence Acquisition: We conducted a structured thematic narrative review of peer-reviewed and grey literature published from January 2010 to March 2025, reported in accordance with PRISMA 2020 and PRISMA-ScR. PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science were searched from February 5 to 18, 2025. Search keywords combined three concept blocks: (1) Digital-health technologies (artificial intelligence, machine learning, mHealth, eHealth, telemedicine, electronic health records, wearables, wastewater surveillance, genomic surveillance, natural-language processing); (2) infectious diseases (outbreak, pandemic, epidemic, COVID-19, Ebola, Zika, mpox, influenza, antimicrobial resistance, Marburg); and (3) public-health functions (surveillance, detection, forecasting, contact tracing, governance, equity). Full Boolean syntax is provided. Records were deduplicated in EndNote X20 and screened by 2 blinded reviewers in Rayyan (Cohen κ = 0.81). Empirical studies were appraised using the Mixed Methods Appraisal Tool 2018, and reviews were appraised using Joanna Briggs Institute checklists. The synthesis followed Braun and Clarke's reflexive thematic analysis and was interpreted through sociotechnical systems and data justice lenses. Results: Of 1498 records, 142 studies met the inclusion criteria; 48% (69/142) were rated as high quality. The evidence base was heavily skewed regionally (high-income countries [HICs], 41%; sub-Saharan Africa or South Asia, 19%). No technology was universally superior. Artificial intelligence (AI) studies were concentrated in HICs (18/32, 56%), whereas short-message-service (SMS) mHealth studies were concentrated in low- and middle-income countries (LMICs; 15/25, 60%). Recurring barriers included algorithmic bias, fragmented interoperability, donor-dependent sustainability, and slow benefit-sharing of pathogen data with LMICs. Conclusions: Across 142 included studies the synthesis supports a measured conclusion: Digital health is necessary but not sufficient for pandemic preparedness. The patterns most consistently observed in the evidence — regional skew, recurring governance failures, donor-dependent sustainability, and design choices that determine equity outcomes — together indicate that equity, sustainability, and accountability are constituents of effectiveness rather than obstacles to it. The binding constraint is increasingly political and institutional will rather than analytic uncertainty.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines digital health innovations for pandemic preparedness, focusing on infectious disease surveillance, with a central question on how these technologies address equity gaps and governance challenges in the context of recent pandemics including avian influenza A(H5N1).
Evidence base and method
A structured thematic narrative review of peer-reviewed and grey literature (January 2010–March 2025) following PRISMA 2020 and PRISMA-ScR, searching PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science (February 5–18, 2025). Included 142 studies (48% high quality), appraised using Mixed Methods Appraisal Tool 2018 and Joanna Briggs Institute checklists, with synthesis via Braun and Clarke's reflexive thematic analysis.
Consensus and conclusions
Digital health is necessary but not sufficient for pandemic preparedness. Regional skew (41% HICs, 19% sub-Saharan Africa/South Asia), governance failures, donor-dependent sustainability, and inequitable design choices are consistent patterns. Equity, sustainability, and accountability are integral to effectiveness, with political will as the binding constraint.
Controversies and gaps
No technology is universally superior; AI studies are concentrated in HICs (56%), while SMS mHealth dominates LMICs (60%). Recurring barriers include algorithmic bias, fragmented interoperability, slow benefit-sharing of pathogen data with LMICs, and regional underrepresentation.
Research and practice implications
Prioritize addressing regional inequities, improving interoperability, ensuring sustainable funding beyond donor dependence, and enhancing data-sharing mechanisms with LMICs. Strengthen governance frameworks to embed equity and accountability in digital health design for pandemic preparedness.

协商安全性和弹性:英国家禽系统 禽流感 控制的规划难题

Negotiating security and resilience: Planning conundrums for avian influenza control in Great Britain's poultry systems
作者： Mathew Hennessey, Paniz Hosseini, James L. N. Wood, Pablo Alarcon, Ivo Syndicus, Pablo Alarcón
摘要中文翻译摘要 2021 年至 2026 年英国 高致病性禽流感 的 暴发 代表了该国有记录的病毒活动最严重的时期,促使人们重新审视如何在家禽系统中概念化和实施生物安全。本文研究了生产肉类、蛋类和猎禽的家禽部门的结构如何影响疾病风险以及弹性家禽生态的可能性。我们采用生产和分销网络方法,将对行业、政策、灰色和已发表文献的案头审查与来自 19 次深度访谈的定性数据结合起来,并辅以实地考察和参与利益相关者活动。我们的分析表明,高度集成和地理集中的家禽肉和蛋鸡系统与野生和半 野生鸟类 相互作用,产生了独特的挑战。生产和加工基础设施的集中、住房存量老化、消费者对散养的需求以及野禽的大规模释放影响着家禽治理的决策以及 禽流感 入侵的风险问题。我们确定了影响 禽流感 病毒脆弱性的两个交叉“纠缠”:(1)家禽规划,其中分散且经常有争议的土地利用决策与环境和生物多样性政策相互作用,以塑造野生和半野生生态的暴露程度;(2) 家禽保险,其中私人 禽流感 保险的市场失灵以及不平衡且有争议的国家赔偿制度在生产者和部门之间分配了不同的风险。我们认为,农场层面的生物安全虽然必要,但不足以在近乎流行的 禽流感 病毒压力下确保英国的家禽生产。更具生物弹性的家禽未来将需要宏观层面的多部门协调,将空间规划、基础设施更新和疾病防备的公平风险分担机制结合起来。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究重点关注英国的 高致病性禽流感 暴发(2021-2026 年),研究家禽业结构(肉、蛋、猎禽)如何影响疾病风险和生态恢复力。核心问题是如何在近乎流行的病毒压力下改进生物安全概念和实践。
证据基础与方法
本文采用生产和分销网络方法,将对行业、政策、灰色和已发表文献的案头审查与 19 次深度访谈的定性数据相结合,并辅以实地访问和利益相关者参与。
共识与结论
高度集成和地理集中的家禽系统与野生/半 野生鸟类 相互作用,带来了独特的挑战。两个交叉的“纠缠”形成了脆弱性:(1) 家禽规划,其中分散的土地使用决策与环境政策相互作用;(2) 保险机制,其中市场失灵和不均匀的国家补偿不公平地分配风险。仅农场一级的生物安全是不够的。
争议与缺口
该分析确定了消费者对散养产品的需求、老化的住房存量和大规模野鸟放归之间的紧张关系,这使治理决策变得复杂。没有报告明确的方法学限制,但对定性数据的依赖可能会限制普遍性。
科研与实践启示
具有生物抵抗力的家禽未来需要宏观层面的多部门协调,整合空间规划、基础设施更新和疾病预防的公平风险分担机制。

Negotiating security and resilience: Planning conundrums for avian influenza control in Great Britain's poultry systems

Authors: Mathew Hennessey, Paniz Hosseini, James L. N. Wood, Pablo Alarcon, Ivo Syndicus, Pablo Alarcón
Original abstract (English)
Abstract The outbreaks of highly pathogenic avian influenza in Great Britain between 2021 and 2026 represent the most severe period of virus activity recorded in the country, prompting renewed scrutiny of how biosecurity is conceptualised and practised within poultry systems. This paper examines how the structure of poultry sectors, producing meat, eggs, and game birds, shape disease risk and the possibilities for a resilient poultry ecology. Using a production and distribution network approach, we combine a desk-based review of industry, policy, grey, and published literature with qualitative data from 19 in-depth interviews, supplemented by field visits and engagement in stakeholder events. Our analysis shows that highly integrated and geographically concentrated poultry meat and layer systems interact with wild and semi-wild birds in ways which generate distinct challenges. Concentration of production and processing infrastructure, aging housing stock, consumer demand for free-range, and large-scale releases of game birds impact decisions around poultry governance and questions of risk around avian influenza incursion. We identify two cross-cutting ‘entanglements’ that condition avian influenza virus vulnerability: (1) poultry planning, where fragmented and often contested land-use decisions interact with environmental and biodiversity policies to shape exposure to wild and semi-wild ecologies; and (2) the insuring of poultry, where a market failure in private avian influenza insurance and uneven and contested state compensation regimes differentially distribute risk across producers and sectors. We argue that farm-level biosecurity, while necessary, is insufficient to secure Britain’s poultry production under near-endemic avian influenza virus pressure. A more bio-resilient poultry future will require macro-level, multi-sectoral coordination that integrates spatial planning, infrastructure renewal, and equitable risk-sharing mechanisms for disease preparedness.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on highly pathogenic avian influenza outbreaks in Great Britain (2021-2026), examining how poultry sector structures (meat, eggs, game birds) shape disease risk and ecological resilience. The central question is how biosecurity conceptualization and practice can be improved under near-endemic virus pressure.
Evidence base and method
The paper employs a production and distribution network approach, combining a desk-based review of industry, policy, grey, and published literature with qualitative data from 19 in-depth interviews, supplemented by field visits and stakeholder engagement.
Consensus and conclusions
Highly integrated and geographically concentrated poultry systems interact with wild/semi-wild birds, creating distinct challenges. Two cross-cutting 'entanglements' shape vulnerability: (1) poultry planning, where fragmented land-use decisions interact with environmental policies, and (2) insurance mechanisms, where market failures and uneven state compensation distribute risk inequitably. Farm-level biosecurity alone is insufficient.
Controversies and gaps
The analysis identifies tensions between consumer demand for free-range products, aging housing stock, and large-scale game bird releases, which complicate governance decisions. No explicit methodological limitations are reported, but the reliance on qualitative data may limit generalizability.
Research and practice implications
A bio-resilient poultry future requires macro-level, multi-sectoral coordination integrating spatial planning, infrastructure renewal, and equitable risk-sharing mechanisms for disease preparedness.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

保护我们的本土物种免受高致病性禽流感(HPAI)的侵害 |新西兰政府 - 第一产业部

Protecting our native species from HPAI (bird flu) | NZ Government - Ministry for Primary Industries
新闻简报新西兰第一产业部已确认在该国境内个体 野生鸟类 中检测到禽流感 (HPAI)。为此,当局建立了热线电话和在线报告系统,供公民报告三人或三人以上患病或死亡的群体 野生鸟类。政府正在向公众提供有关如何限制病毒影响并为潜在的进一步传播做好准备的指导。虽然在个别鸟类中证实了这一检测,但尚未披露具体的病例数、死亡人数或更广泛的风险评估。重点仍然是 监测、报告机制和保护本土物种的公共教育。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-23。证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
受影响人群:野生鸟类。
事件
新西兰 野生鸟类 个体中确认检测到禽流感 (HPAI)。由第一产业部报告。
规模、影响与风险
案例:个人野生鸟类。没有报告具体病例数、死亡数或暴露数。风险:证据中未明确说明。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生应对措施包括建立外来病虫害热线(0800 80 99 66)和在线报告门户网站(report.mpi.govt.nz),用于报告群体中 3 名或以上患病或死亡的 野生鸟类。提供了关于限制禽流感影响和做好准备的指南。没有有关调查、测试、隔离、接触者追踪或 治疗 状态的信息。来源限制:已审查完整的原始报告正文。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Protecting our native species from HPAI (bird flu) | NZ Government - Ministry for Primary Industries

News Brief
The New Zealand Ministry for Primary Industries has confirmed the detection of bird flu (HPAI) in individual wild birds within the country. In response, authorities have established a hotline and online reporting system for citizens to report groups of three or more sick or dead wild birds. The government is providing guidance to the public on how to limit the impact of the virus and be prepared for potential further spread. While the detection is confirmed in individual birds, no specific case numbers, deaths, or broader risk assessments have been disclosed. The focus remains on surveillance, reporting mechanisms, and public education to protect native species.

禽流感 每周更新#1053:2026 年 7 月 18 日 - 世界卫生组织 (WHO)

Avian Influenza Weekly Update # 1053: 18 July 2026 - World Health Organization (WHO)
新闻简报世界卫生组织 (WHO) 西太平洋区域于 2026 年 7 月 18 日发布了 禽流感 每周更新(禽流感 每周更新#1053)。本报告重点关注 禽流感 A 病毒,该病毒主要在鸟类中传播,但也可以通过直接接触受感染的家禽或受污染的环境来感染人类。虽然这些病毒目前不易在人与人之间传播,但它们在家禽中的持续传播因其潜在的疾病、死亡和 突变 的可能性而引起关注。该更新强调了 禽流感 区域响应的最新进展,强调了监测和准备的重要性。

发布日期:2026 年 7 月 18 日。事件日期:所提供的证据中未明确说明。

事件
关于 禽流感 A 病毒的每周情况报告,重点介绍了区域应对措施的最新进展。确认 禽流感 A 亚型(HxNy)通常在鸟类中传播,但也可以感染人类。
规模、影响与风险
病例、死亡和暴露:所提供的证据中未报告。风险:禽流感 病毒在家禽中的持续传播令人担忧,因为有可能导致轻度至重度疾病、死亡,并且 突变 的传染性更强。
应对、状态与不确定性

A high priorityPublic-health news

Avian Influenza Weekly Update # 1053: 18 July 2026 - World Health Organization (WHO)

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) Western Pacific Region has released its weekly update on avian influenza (Avian Influenza Weekly Update #1053) on 18 July 2026. This report focuses on avian influenza A viruses, which primarily spread among birds but can also infect humans through direct contact with infected poultry or contaminated environments. While these viruses do not currently transmit easily between people, their ongoing circulation in poultry poses a concern due to the potential for illness, death, and mutation. The update highlights recent developments in the regional response to avian influenza, emphasizing the importance of monitoring and preparedness.
Original source text (English)

in birds but can also infect humans. B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 Bird Flu Has Now Hit Over 1,166 U.S. Dairy Herds Across 19 States, With Idaho Leading the Nation - Medical Daily

News Brief
H5N1 bird flu has spread to over 1,166 U.S. dairy herds across 19 states, with Idaho reporting the highest number of cases. The outbreak, confirmed by Medical Daily, represents a significant spread of the virus among dairy cattle. While the exact response measures and current status are not detailed in the provided evidence, the scale of the outbreak underscores the need for continued monitoring and intervention to prevent further transmission and potential impact on the agricultural sector and public health.

新西兰发现首例H5N1禽流感病例,启动濒危物种疫苗接种 - AOL.com

New Zealand detects first H5N1 bird flu case, starts vaccination of endangered species - AOL.com
新闻简报新西兰报告了首例 H5N1 禽流感病例,感染者是在惠灵顿附近发现的一只棕色贼鸥海鸟。生物安全部长安德鲁·霍加德(Andrew Hoggard)证实了这一发现,强调没有证据表明野生动物中存在大规模死亡率或野生鸟类之间的传播,家禽中也没有病例。由于高脆弱性,该国启动了一项 疫苗接种 计划,针对来自 5 种濒危物种的 300 只核心种鸟,其中包括高鹤和鸮鹦鹉。卫生官员担心如果病毒迅速传播,可能存在灭绝风险。新西兰借鉴邻国澳大利亚所做的准备,正在增加 监测 和检测,澳大利亚已报告 14 例确诊或推定阳性 H5 禽流感病例。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-22。事件日期:2026年7月15日(据生物安全部长安德鲁·霍加德报告)。
地点与对象
新西兰,特别是惠灵顿。受影响的种群:棕色贼鸥海鸟。面临风险的濒危物种包括不会飞的鸮鹮和鸮鹦鹉。
事件
新西兰确认首例 H5N1 禽流感病例,是在一只候鸟中发现的。生物安全部长安德鲁·霍加德报告了此案。没有证据表明野生动物中存在 死亡率 或 野生鸟类 之间的 传播,在家禽中也没有检测到。
规模、影响与风险
棕色贼鸥海鸟确诊一例。没有报告 野生鸟类 之间的质量 死亡率 或 传播。家禽中未检出。由于濒危物种的脆弱性,它们面临的风险很高,卫生官员担心如果病毒迅速传播,它们可能会灭绝。
应对、状态与不确定性
新西兰一直在为 H5N1 的到来做好准备,与家禽业合作制定生物安全计划。增加了监测并正在进行测试。针对来自 5 个濒危物种的 300 只核心种鸟的 疫苗接种 计划已经启动。疫苗 的有效性不确定,因为鸟类尚未接触过该病毒。试验中没有观察到不良副作用。

New Zealand detects first H5N1 bird flu case, starts vaccination of endangered species - AOL.com

News Brief
New Zealand has reported its first case of H5N1 bird flu in a brown skua seabird found near Wellington. Biosecurity Minister Andrew Hoggard confirmed the detection, emphasizing no evidence of mass mortality in wildlife or transmission between wild birds, and no cases in poultry. The country has initiated a vaccination program for 300 core breeding birds from five endangered species, including takahe and kakapo, due to their high vulnerability. Health officials are concerned about potential extinction risks if the virus spreads rapidly. New Zealand is increasing surveillance and testing, drawing on preparations made in neighboring Australia, which has reported 14 confirmed or presumed positive H5 bird flu cases.
Original source text (English)
WELLINGTON, July 15 (Reuters) - New Zealand reported its first case of the deadly H5N1 bird flu after a migratory seabird found on a beach near the capital, Wellington, tested positive, Biosecurity Minister Andrew Hoggard said on Wednesday. The case in a brown skua seabird comes after the virus was detected last month in Australia, the last continent to report the virus. The virulent H5 strain has spread through wild bird and mammal populations since 2021, killing millions and infecting poultry and dairy farms as well as some farmworkers. "There is no evidence of any mass mortality in wildlife or transmission between wild birds in New Zealand. There has been no detection in poultry," Hoggard said in a statement. Hoggard said New Zealand may see a similar pattern to cases reported in neighbouring Australia, where there have been 14 confirmed or presumed positive detections of H5 bird flu as of Wednesday. New Zealand has been preparing for the arrival of H5N1, working with the poultry industry to develop biosecurity and resilience plans. "We're going to be doing more surveillance, more testing, but by and large it's carrying on with the same work with the industry we've been doing so far," Hoggard said at a press conference. Many of New Zealand's endemic birds are unusually vulnerable because they evolved for millions of years without native land mammals, leaving many unique species, which are flightless, ground-nesting and poorly equipped to defend themselves against predators. That isolation made them unique but also left them highly exposed to habitat loss and introduced pests such as stoats, rats and feral cats. Many species are already endangered, putting them at great risk of extinction if the virus spreads rapidly. Health officials have started a vaccination programme for 300 core breeding birds from five of the country's most endangered species, including flightless takahe and kakapo. Hoggard said they had trialed the vaccine last year and had seen no adverse side effects but could not be certain how effective it was as the birds had not been exposed to the virus. Brett Gartrell, professor of wildlife health at Massey University, told Reuters he was worried that bird flu could push some of the country's critically endangered birds to extinction. "We are incredibly worried about New Zealand's biodiversity because our birds have never had to deal with anything like this before," Gartrell said. "If it spreads quickly in New Zealand, we could be in trouble," he said. (Reporting by Lucy Craymer in Wellington and Renju Jose in Sydney; Editing by Kate Mayberry, Sonali Paul and Lincoln Feast.)
View five news elements
Time
Publication date: 2026-07-22. Event date: July 15, 2026 (as reported by Biosecurity Minister Andrew Hoggard).
Location and population
New Zealand, specifically Wellington. Affected population: A brown skua seabird. Endangered species at risk include flightless takahe and kakapo.
Event
Confirmed first case of H5N1 bird flu in New Zealand, detected in a migratory seabird. Biosecurity Minister Andrew Hoggard reported the case. No evidence of mass mortality in wildlife or transmission between wild birds, and no detection in poultry.
Scale, impact and risk
One confirmed case in a brown skua seabird. No mass mortality or transmission between wild birds reported. No detection in poultry. Risk to endangered species is high due to their vulnerability, with health officials expressing concern about potential extinction if the virus spreads rapidly.
Response, status and uncertainty
New Zealand has been preparing for H5N1 arrival, working with the poultry industry on biosecurity plans. Increased surveillance and testing are being conducted. A vaccination program for 300 core breeding birds from five endangered species has started. Vaccine effectiveness is uncertain as the birds have not been exposed to the virus. No adverse side effects were observed in trials.

# H5N1 已经到来:澳大利亚禽流感自由的结束对家禽生产商意味着什么 - Lockton

# H5N1 has arrived: What the end of Australia's Bird Flu freedom means for poultry producers - Lockton
新闻简报2026 年 6 月 20 日报告首次在野生海鸟中确认检测到高致病性 H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b,澳大利亚因此失去了 H5N1 禽流感 的状态。截至 7 月 15 日,西澳大利亚州、南澳大利亚州和新南威尔士州已发现 14 例病例,全部位于 野生鸟类,迄今为止尚未出现商业家禽感染。这标志着一个重大转变,因为该菌株比澳大利亚之前成功遏制的高致病性禽流感入侵更具传染性和持久性。虽然目前没有贸易限制,但已建立的 野生鸟类 储存宿主 提高了家禽生产商的暴露风险。行业的应对措施包括加强生物安全措施,英厄姆等公司实施封锁,并为鸡群寻求住房订单。当局强调通过紧急动物疾病热线尽早报告,并强调业务连续性计划的重要性,因为政府补偿不涵盖生产损失或更广泛的收入影响。暴发 响应者的社会心理损失也被认为是工作场所安全义务中的一个重要考虑因素。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-22。事件日期:2026 年 6 月 20 日,检测截至 2026 年 7 月 15 日仍在继续。
地点与对象
澳大利亚,特别是西澳大利亚州(大角国家公园、鹰巢)、南澳大利亚州和新南威尔士州。受影响种群:野生海鸟(14 种已确认或推测呈阳性检测)。迄今为止没有商业家禽感染的报道。
事件
确认在澳大利亚野生海鸟中检测到高致病性 H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b,结束了该国对该毒株的自由。由官方消息来源和行业利益相关者报道。事件已确认并正在进行中。
规模、影响与风险
14 confirmed or presumed positive detections in wild birds across three states. No commercial poultry infections, mass mortality events, or trade restrictions reported. Global context: Over 2,000 HPAI outbreaks in 64 countries (2025-2026), with 140+ million birds lost. Risk assessment: Increased local transmission risk due to established wild bird reservoir; potential for spillover to mammals and commercial poultry; heightened trade sensitivity.
应对、状态与不确定性
行业通过加强生物安全措施来应对(例如,Inghams 锁定业务、寻求住房令)。政府补偿计划涵盖直接扑杀/处置成本,但不涵盖更广泛的业务中断。生产商建议加强生物安全、监控 野生鸟类 接触、尽早报告并审查保险。在 WHS 职责中强调对劳动力的社会心理支持至关重要。来源限制:已审查完整的原始文章(content_status:完整)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

# H5N1 has arrived: What the end of Australia's Bird Flu freedom means for poultry producers - Lockton

News Brief
Australia has lost its status as free from H5N1 avian influenza, with the first confirmed detection of highly pathogenic H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b in wild seabirds reported on 20 June 2026. As of 15 July, 14 cases have been identified across Western Australia, South Australia, and New South Wales, all in wild birds with no commercial poultry infections to date. This marks a significant shift, as the strain is more transmissible and persistent than previous HPAI incursions Australia has successfully contained. While no trade restrictions are currently in place, the established wild bird reservoir elevates exposure risks for poultry producers. Industry responses include enhanced biosecurity measures, with companies like Inghams implementing lockdowns and seeking housing orders for flocks. Authorities emphasize early reporting via the Emergency Animal Disease Hotline and stress the importance of business continuity planning, as government compensation does not cover lost production or broader revenue impacts. The psychosocial toll on outbreak responders is also noted as a critical consideration under workplace safety obligations.
Original source text (English)
ARTICLES / JULY 21, 2026 # H5N1 has arrived: What the end of Australia's Bird Flu freedom means for poultry producers Until June 2026, Australia held a distinction no other continent could claim: complete freedom from the H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b strain of avian influenza that has devastated poultry flocks across Europe, Asia, Africa and the Americas since 2021. That distinction has now ended. On 20 June 2026, a vagrant migratory seabird found in Cape Le Grand National Park on Western Australia's southern coast tested positive for high pathogenicity H5N1. As at 15 July 2026, Australia has 14 confirmed or presumed positive detections in wild birds: eight in Western Australia, five in South Australia and one in New South Wales, most recently a giant petrel found near Hawks Nest. All detections to date are in wild seabirds. There is no evidence of infection in commercial poultry, no evidence of mass mortality events and currently no trade restrictions on Australian poultry or poultry product exports. For poultry producers, this is not cause for panic, but it is a genuine inflection point. Australia's biosecurity system has successfully contained every prior HPAI incursion, including the H7N3 and H7N9 outbreaks that hit eight Victorian properties in 2024 and the H7N8 outbreak that affected four Victorian properties in early 2025. Both were eradicated within months. H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b, however, behaves differently to the H7 strains Australia has dealt with before. Globally, it has proven more transmissible in wild bird populations, more persistent, and more capable of spilling into mammals. The World Organisation for Animal Health recorded more than 2,000 HPAI outbreaks across 64 countries between 2025 and 2026, resulting in the loss or culling of over 140 million birds. Why this changes the risk calculation The reservoir is now local Wild seabirds carrying the virus are established along Australia's southern and western coastlines. Migratory bird pathways and proximity to wetlands, estuaries and coastal roosting sites are now a live exposure factor for any producer, not a theoretical one. 2. Industry is already responding. Inghams, Australia's largest poultry producer, has locked down non-essential access to its Western Australian operations and sought a regional housing order to bring free-range flocks indoors. Producers in WA and SA in particular should expect this pattern to spread as detections continue. 3. Detection does not equal containment cost. A single infected commercial shed can trigger movement restrictions, culling and quarantine well beyond that property, affecting neighbouring producers, hatcheries, transport contractors and processors who never had a sick bird on site. 4. Trade sensitivity is heightened. Australia's clean status has long been a competitive advantage in export markets. The first confirmed poultry infection, even if swiftly contained, is likely to trigger a period of heightened scrutiny from trading partners and potential short-term restrictions while status is reassessed. What producers should be doing now Tighten biosecurity to outbreak-ready standard Vehicle disinfection points, restricted shed access, dedicated PPE and strict control of movement between sites are no longer precautionary extras. Where feasible, consider housing orders or netting for free-range and outdoor-access flocks, particularly in WA, SA and coastal NSW. 2. Deter and monitor wild bird contact. Covered feed and water sources, netting over outdoor ranges and active discouragement of wild waterfowl on or near production sites reduce the single biggest transmission pathway. 3. Report early, without exception. Any unusual mortality, respiratory signs or drop in egg production should go straight to the Emergency Animal Disease Hotline (1800 675 888). Early detection is the difference between a contained incident and a prolonged one. 4. Review insurance now, not after a detection. Livestock mortality and business interruption capacity for avian influenza contracts sharply once an outbreak reaches commercial poultry. Cover secured ahead of a confirmed infection is materially easier to place and typically better priced than cover sought during an active event. 5. Understand what government compensation does and does not cover. Compensation schemes generally address the direct cost of culling and disposal. They do not typically extend to lost production, lost contracts, restocking delays, or the broader revenue interruption that follows a quarantine period. That gap is where a well-structured business interruption policy can do its work. The people risk: managing stress in your own workforce The financial and operational consequences of an outbreak are well understood. The impact on the people who have to manage it is less often discussed and deserves equal weight in planning. Mass depopulation is traumatic work. Overseas experience from major HPAI and FMD events consistently shows elevated rates of acute stress, sleep disturbance and longer-term psychological impact among the staff, contract cullers and vets directly involved in culling and disposal. This is not a fringe consideration. It is a predictable outcome of the work itself. 2. Owner-operators carry a compounding load. Financial uncertainty while awaiting compensation, regulatory scrutiny, movement restrictions that cut off normal social contact, and public or media attention can pile on the practical demands of running an outbreak response. Family-run operations, in particular, have no separation between business and personal stress. 3. This now sits within your WHS duty of care. Psychosocial hazards, including exposure to traumatic events and high job demand with low support, are explicitly covered under Australia's model WHS laws. An outbreak response plan that addresses biosecurity and logistics but not the psychosocial exposure of the people executing it is incomplete from a compliance standpoint, not just a well-being one. 4. Build workforce support into the response plan itself, not as an afterthought. Practical measures can include rotating staff involved in culling and disposal to limit cumulative exposure, engaging EAP or trained counselling support ahead of and during the event rather than only after, keeping communication with staff clear and honest to reduce uncertainty-driven anxiety, and involving HR or WHS leads in outbreak planning alongside the biosecurity and insurance teams. 5. Check whether your cover reflects this. Business Interruption cover can also do double duty here: removing financial uncertainty as a live variable during an active event reduces one of the biggest stressors for owner-operators. The bottom line Australia's poultry sector has a strong track record of containing and eradicating avian influenza outbreaks. What has changed is the nature of the threat. For the first time, H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b has been detected in Australia's wild bird population, bringing a risk profile that is more persistent, more unpredictable and potentially more disruptive than previous HPAI incursions. While there is currently no evidence of infection in commercial poultry, producers now face a different operating environment. The challenge is no longer simply preventing an outbreak on-site. It is understanding how a detection on a neighbouring property, within a nearby wild bird colony, or elsewhere in the supply chain could affect operations, workforce wellbeing, contractual obligations and financial performance. The businesses best positioned to respond will likely be those that act before a commercial detection occurs. That means reviewing biosecurity measures, testing outbreak response plans, addressing workforce and psychosocial risks, and ensuring insurance and business continuity arrangements reflect the realities of a prolonged disruption. In a landscape where the risk has changed, preparedness remains one or the most effective forms of protection. Don't wait until capacity tightens Following a confirmed commercial outbreak, insurance availability and underwriting appetite can change quickly. Now is the time to review your livestock mortality, business interruption and broader risk management arrangements. Contact our Protein Practice team for more information, or review of your current insurance and risk management program. The contents of this publication are provided for general information only. Risk assessment: Increased local transmission risk due to established wild bird reservoir; potential for spillover to mammals and commercial poultry; heightened trade sensitivity.

Industry responding with enhanced biosecurity measures (e.g., Inghams locking down operations, seeking housing orders). 研究将越南的家禽网络与 H9N2 患病率 联系起来 - avinews.com

Study links poultry networks to H9N2 prevalence in Vietnam - avinews.com
新闻简报伦敦皇家兽医学院 Mathew Hennessey 博士及其团队的一项研究表明,越南北部的家禽生产和分销网络配置显着影响 H9N2 禽流感 病毒 患病率 和人类暴露风险。这项研究于 2021 年 3 月至 2022 年 3 月在北江省、河内省、海阳省和广宁省进行,发现混合多种来源的家禽和紧张的运输条件等做法会放大生产和分销网络 (PDN) 上的病毒性循环。地方性 H9N2 病毒造成直接的人畜共患病风险,并导致新病毒重组。虽然越南的禽流感和人类流感防范计划侧重于升级批发市场和工业屠宰场,但非正式屠宰点仍然广泛使用,这凸显了对小型非正式分销设施制定有针对性的 监测 和风险缓解策略的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-07-20;研究于2021年3月至2022年3月进行。
地点与对象
越南北部,特别是北江省、河内省、海阳省和广宁省;家禽生产和分销网络。
事件
伦敦皇家兽医学院的 Mathew Hennessey 和他的团队发现,家禽生产和分销网络配置会影响越南的 H9N2 禽流感 病毒 患病率 和人类暴露风险。
规模、影响与风险
H9N2 病毒在越南流行;较高的 患病率 与混合来自多个来源的家禽等做法相关;生产和分销网络 (PDN) 上的病毒式流通放大反映了交易状况;直接或通过新型病毒重组造成人畜共患风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
越南禽流感和人类流感防范计划优先考虑升级批发市场和工业屠宰场,但非正式屠宰点仍然是首选;监测 和风险缓解策略必须针对众多小型、非正式的配送设施;农场和市场的 患病率 数据差距限制了对 传播 风险的理解。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Study links poultry networks to H9N2 prevalence in Vietnam - avinews.com

News Brief
A study by Dr. Mathew Hennessey and his team from the Royal Veterinary College, London, reveals that poultry production and distribution network configurations in northern Vietnam significantly influence H9N2 avian influenza virus prevalence and human exposure risk. Conducted from March 2021 to March 2022 across Bac Giang, Hanoi, Hai Duong, and Quang Ninh Provinces, the research found that practices like mixing poultry from multiple sources and stressful transportation conditions amplify viral circulation along the production and distribution network (PDN). The endemic H9N2 viruses pose direct zoonotic risks and contribute to novel virus reassortments. While Vietnam's Avian and Human Influenza Preparedness program focuses on upgrading wholesale markets and industrial slaughterhouses, informal slaughter points remain widely used, highlighting the need for targeted surveillance and risk mitigation strategies for small, informal distribution facilities.

READ SUMMARY SUBSCRIBE RECEIVE IN PAPER OTHER EDITIONS Back 21 Jul 2026 Study links poultry networks to H9N2 prevalence in Vietnam Low pathogenicity avian influenza A subtype H9N2 viruses are endemic in Vietnam. Arief Fachrudin aviNews Asia SAVE IN THE LIBRARY PDF FILE Animal Health & PathologyResearchAvian influenzaH9n2 Poultry production and distribution network configurations, including the origins of supplying farms, influence H9N2 avian influenza virus prevalence and thus human exposure risk, results from a study indicated. The study was performed by Dr Mathew Hennessey of the Royal Veterinary College in London, UK and his team of researchers. Figure 1. Geographic area and sites for study of avian influenza virus A(H9N2) prevalence across chicken production and distribution networks, Bac Giang, Hanoi, Hai Duong, and Quang Ninh Provinces, Vietnam, March 2021–March 2022. The 4 study provinces and the individual sites (farms and distribution facilities) are shown alongside the detection of avian influenza viruses (contaminated defined as >1 positive sample; noncontaminated defined as all samples testing negative) and the spatiotemporal cluster of contaminated farms. The 2 other farms that appear located in the cluster were not sampled during the temporal window. A cross-sectional study in northern Vietnam Their research paper explained that high-pathogenicity avian influenza A subtype H5N1 viruses of the Goose/Guangdong/1/96 lineage and low pathogenicity avian influenza A subtype H9N2 viruses are endemic in Southeast Asia, including Vietnam. Both subtypes affect poultry production and pose zoonotic risks directly and through their involvement in the generation of novel virus reassortments. In Vietnam, the subtypes are frequently detected in live bird markets, and higher prevalence is associated with practices such as mixing poultry from multiple sources. Figure 2. Time-scaled phylogenies of hemagglutinin sampled avian influenza virus A(H9N2) virus genomes in study of H9N2 virus prevalence across chicken production and distribution networks, Bac Giang, Hanoi, Hai Duong, and Quang Ninh Provinces, Vietnam, March 2021–March 2022. Tree tips are labeled by the unique sampled site identification; red text indicates sequences from 3 farms in the spatiotemporal cluster. Branches are colored by posterior support, and horizontal node bars represent 95% highest posterior density intervals of node ages. Heatmaps indicate province, site type, and chicken type of each sequence. C, chicken type; P, province; S, site type.Continue after advertising. However, that finding should be interpreted cautiously because farms and the markets they supply have limited prevalence data, and the focus on markets has diverted attention from larger slaughter facilities despite those facilities playing a key role in poultry distribution, the researchers stated. Together, those gaps constrain understanding of avian influenza virus (AIV) transmission risk along the production and distribution network (PDN) through which poultry are raised, traded, and consumed. The researchers conducted a cross-sectional study in northern Vietnam to assess avian influenza A(H5N1) and A(H9N2) prevalence in chickens, determine how AIV prevalence varied between farms and the different distribution facility types, and examine how viral genetic diversity was structured along the PDN. Figure 3. Time-scaled phylogenies of neuraminidase of sampled avian influenza virus A(H9N2) virus genomes in study of H9N2 virus prevalence across chicken production and distribution networks, Bac Giang, Hanoi, Hai Duong, and Quang Ninh Provinces, Vietnam, March 2021–March 2022. Tree tips are labeled by the unique sampled site identification; red text indicates sequences from 3 farms in the spatiotemporal cluster. Branches are colored by posterior support, and horizontal node bars represent 95% highest posterior density intervals of node ages. Heatmaps indicate province, site type, and chicken type of each sequence. C, chicken type; P, province; S site type. Findings from the study The researchers found that poultry production and distribution network configurations, including the origins of supplying farms, influence H9N2 virus prevalence and thus human exposure risk. The pattern of viral circulation amplification along the PDN is consistent with studies in southern Vietnam and Bangladesh and probably reflects trading conditions. For example, birds are mixed from multiple sources and experience lengthy, stressful transportation (for example: crowding and lack of sustenance), creating opportunities for virus exposure and transmission en route; that finding was supported by the improved model fit when they included supplying areas, suggesting that procurement and distribution practices influence viral prevalence at distribution facilities. High viral prevalence in slaughter points and retail markets probably reflects sourcing involving multiple suppliers, including wholesale markets, indicating that chickens spent longer in the PDN before sampling. The lack of geographic structure in AIV genetic diversity further points to extensive viral mixing among poultry populations. Control and surveillance efforts should therefore address the entire poultry PDN. The Vietnam Avian and Human Influenza Preparedness program has prioritized upgrading wholesale markets and industrial slaughterhouses and promoting their use. Although low prevalence in such facilities supports this focus, slaughterhouses remain underused, and informal slaughter points are still preferred. Surveillance and risk mitigation strategies must also target the numerous distribution facilities in Vietnam that are small and informal but widely used. Read full paper of the study here. FFAR 快速资助探索抗病家禽以控制 禽流感 - The Fence Post

FFAR rapid funding to explore disease-resistant poultry for avian influenza control - The Fence Post
新闻简报食品与农业研究基金会 (FFAR) 和加州大学戴维斯分校宣布提供 299,938 美元的农业研究快速成果 (ROAR) 拨款,通过探索家禽的自然抗病能力来对抗 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI)。这项研究由周怀军博士和罗德里戈·加拉多博士领导,重点关注法尤米鸡,这是一种以耐寒而闻名的传统品种。使用鸡胚胎作为研究模型,该团队将比较法尤米鸡、肉鸡和蛋鸡的存活率、病毒水平和 免疫应答。目标是确定与抗病性相关的遗传和生物因素,以开发更具弹性的商业家禽种群。该计划针对造成重大经济损失和粮食供应中断的全球 HPAI 暴发 问题,提供了超越 疫苗 等当前控制措施的潜在长期解决方案。

确认融资公告。食品与农业研究基金会 (FFAR) 和加州大学戴维斯分校正在农业研究快速成果 (ROAR) 拨款中投资 299,938 美元,用于研究抗病家禽以控制 禽流感。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

FFAR rapid funding to explore disease-resistant poultry for avian influenza control - The Fence Post

News Brief
The Foundation for Food & Agriculture Research (FFAR) and University of California, Davis have announced a $299,938 Rapid Outcomes from Agricultural Research (ROAR) grant to combat highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) by exploring natural disease resistance in poultry. This research, led by Dr. Huaijun Zhou and Dr. Rodrigo Gallardo, will focus on Fayoumi chickens, a heritage breed known for hardiness. Using chick embryos as a research model, the team will compare survival rates, virus levels, and immune responses among Fayoumi, broiler, and layer chickens. The goal is to identify genetic and biological factors associated with disease resistance to develop more resilient commercial poultry populations. This initiative addresses global HPAI outbreaks that have caused significant economic losses and disrupted food supplies, offering a potential long-term solution beyond current control measures like vaccines.
Original source text (English)
FFAR rapid funding to explore disease-resistant poultry for avian influenza control News News | Jul 21, 2026 Foundation for Food & Agriculture Research DAVIS, Calif. — Highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) continues to cause major losses for poultry farms around the world, killing millions of commercial and wild birds, disrupting food supplies, and increasing concerns on transmission among multiple animal species. In response, the Foundation for Food & Agriculture Research and the University of California, Davis, are investing $299,938 in a Rapid Outcomes from Agricultural Research grant to identify and harness natural disease resistance in chickens as a new tool to combat avian influenza. Current approaches, including vaccines, can help manage outbreaks. But they face challenges related to availability, speed of deployment and long-term effectiveness. This research project will explore whether some chickens naturally resist infection better than others and how that resistance could strengthen commercial poultry flocks. “Producers need tools to mitigate their losses, and FFAR’s rapid funding is helping to develop the solutions they need,” said Dr. Miriam Martin LeValley, FFAR scientific program manager. “Identifying whether some chickens naturally resist infection could help us craft an effective long-term HPAI response.” FAYOUMI CHICKENS Researchers led by Dr. Huaijun Zhou, Chancellor’s leadership professor and Dr. Rodrigo Gallardo, poultry virologist, at the University of California, Davis, will study the Fayoumi breed, a heritage chicken known for its hardiness and disease resistance. By using eggs containing a developing chick embryo — a safe and efficient research model — the team will compare how Fayoumi, broiler and layer chickens respond to highly pathogenic avian influenza. The researchers will evaluate survival and virus levels across the different breeds. They will also characterize immune responses to infection to identify the genes and biological pathways associated with disease resistance. “HPAI continues to challenge poultry production worldwide, highlighting the need for innovative and sustainable solutions,” said Dr. Zhou. “By identifying the genetic and immune factors that contribute to natural disease resistance, this research could help pave the way for more resilient poultry populations and improved long-term disease control.” Findings from this project could support future breeding programs aimed at enhancing natural resistance to disease in commercial poultry populations. More resilient flocks could reduce economic losses, lessen reliance on emergency disease control measures and strengthen the stability and sustainability of the U.S. food supply. ROAR rapidly funds research and outreach in response to emerging or unanticipated threats to the U.S. food supply or agricultural systems. 研究探索鸟类对 禽流感 的遗传免疫力 - 饲料

Research explores birds’ genetic immunity to avian influenza - Feedstuffs
新闻简报The Foundation for Food and Agriculture Research (FFAR), Iowa State University, and CSIRO have launched a $300,000 research project to identify genetic factors in birds that confer immunity to H5N1 and H7N9 avian influenza viruses. This initiative, funded through the ROAR program, aims to discover genetic targets for new therapies and develop poultry with enhanced natural resistance. The research addresses the significant economic and food security threats posed by HPAI, which has cost the U.S. poultry industry billions in losses. While biosecurity remains the primary defense, the project explores host-directed interventions as a more durable alternative to current virus-focused strategies. Led by Dr. Joaquin Caceres at Iowa State, the team uses advanced genomic tools to understand avian immune responses, supporting the USDA's goal of improving farmer profitability and food system resilience.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-22。事件日期:未明确说明,但研究公告为最新。
地点与对象
美国家禽业。研究机构:爱荷华州立大学、联邦科学与工业研究组织(CSIRO)。资助:美国农业部食品和农业研究基金会 (FFAR)。
事件
A $300,000 research grant funded by FFAR, Iowa State University, and CSIRO to identify genetic factors in birds that influence immune response to H5N1 and H7N9 avian influenza viruses. The project aims to find genetic targets for new therapies and develop poultry with greater natural resistance.
规模、影响与风险
高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 继续威胁美国家禽业,造成数十亿美元的经济损失,扰乱粮食生产,并增加农民和消费者的成本。该研究解决了对生物安全措施之外的新方法的需求。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究正在进行中,利用先进的基因组工具和新颖的实验室模型来了解鸟类对 H5N1 和 H7N9 感染的反应。它支持美国农业部提高农民盈利能力的研究重点,旨在为近期和长期保护家禽群提供替代工具。没有描述当前的 暴发 或立即响应;重点是未来的预防战略。

Research explores birds’ genetic immunity to avian influenza - Feedstuffs

News Brief
The Foundation for Food and Agriculture Research (FFAR), Iowa State University, and CSIRO have launched a $300,000 research project to identify genetic factors in birds that confer immunity to H5N1 and H7N9 avian influenza viruses. This initiative, funded through the ROAR program, aims to discover genetic targets for new therapies and develop poultry with enhanced natural resistance. The research addresses the significant economic and food security threats posed by HPAI, which has cost the U.S. poultry industry billions in losses. While biosecurity remains the primary defense, the project explores host-directed interventions as a more durable alternative to current virus-focused strategies. Led by Dr. Joaquin Caceres at Iowa State, the team uses advanced genomic tools to understand avian immune responses, supporting the USDA's goal of improving farmer profitability and food system resilience.
Original source text (English)

Advancing stewardship and health outcomesSwine Respiratory Disease susceptibility surveillance: Advancing stewardship and health outcomesAug 20, 202612:00 pm CDTNutrition & HealthFFAR project to strengthen U.S. screwworm preparednessFFAR project to strengthen U.S. screwworm preparednessbyFoundation for Food & Agriculture ResearchJul 23, 20262 Min ReadFeedstuffs 365searchclosePoultry Health and NutritionNutrition & HealthResearchResearch explores birds’ genetic immunity to avian influenzaFFAR, Iowa State and CSIRO invest $300K in grant to identify genes involved in immune response to H5N1 and H7N9 infections and find promising targets for new therapies to protect flocks.Foundation for Food & Agriculture ResearchJuly 22, 2026schedule2 Min ReadU.S. Department of AgricultureHighly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) continues to threaten the U.S. poultry industry, causing billions of dollars in economic losses while disrupting food production and increasing costs for farmers and consumers.The Foundation for Food & Agriculture Research (FFAR), Iowa State University and the Commonwealth Scientific and Industrial Research Organization (CSIRO) are investing $300,000 through the Rapid Outcomes from Agricultural Research (ROAR) program to identify genetic factors in birds that are relevant in the host response against H5N1 and H7N9 avian influenza viruses. This research aims to illuminate pathways to modulate the expression of specific host genes to mitigate the avian influenza burden.Although biosecurity measures remain the primary means of combating avian influenza, developments since 2022 demonstrate the need for new approaches. There is an increasing request for the deployment of vaccines; however, concerns remain about vaccine effectiveness and virus evolution.Related:New pathway for B cell development in chickens discovered“We must explore every possible tool in the toolbox to protect producers and their flocks. Unlocking genetic mechanisms that naturally allow birds to resist infection could pave the way for innovative approaches that better protect flocks, strengthen food security and protect producers’ profitability,” said Miriam Martin LeValley, Ph.D., FFAR scientific program manager.This research is identifying genes involved in birds’ immune responses that influence how the virus infects and replicates. Understanding the role of genetics could better inform treatment protocols, improve disease resistance and strengthen future disease prevention strategies.Led by Dr. Joaquin Caceres, assistant professor in the Department of Veterinary Microbiology and Preventive Medicine, College of Veterinary Medicine, at Iowa State University, the multidisciplinary research team is using advanced genomic tools and novel laboratory models to better understand how birds respond to H5N1 and H7N9 infections.The project is identifying promising genetic targets for new therapies, supporting the development of next-generation vaccines and laying the foundation for breeding poultry with greater natural resistance to avian influenza.This FFAR-funded research supports the U.S. Department of Agriculture’s research priority on Increasing Profitability of Farmers and Ranchers. This research aims to provide alternative tools for protecting poultry flocks in the near- and long-term future.FFAR’s ROAR program rapidly funds research and outreach in response to emerging or unanticipated threats to the U.S. food supply or agricultural systems.Related:Social dynamics play key role in broiler breeder reproductive success“Influenza viruses, like any other virus, are obligate intracellular parasites. Therefore, if we modify the host environment, that must have consequences for the virus,” said Dr. Caceres. “Most current mitigation efforts against influenza are against the virus itself, but the virus has evolved multiple elegant ways to circumvent these strategies. Host-directed interventions may offer a more durable approach because host genetic pathways are substantially more stable than the viral genome.”.FFAR builds public-private partnerships to fund bold research addressing big food and agriculture challenges, matching federal funding from Congress with private funding. FFAR was established in the 2014 Farm Bill to increase public agriculture research investments, fill knowledge gaps and complement USDA’s research agenda. Through collaboration and partnerships, FFAR advances actionable science benefiting farmers, consumers and the environment.About the AuthorFoundation for Food & Agriculture ResearchSee more from Foundation for Food & Agriculture Researchchevron_rightSubscribe to Our NewslettersFeedstuffs is the news source for animal agricultureSIGN ME UPcheck_circleEditors ChoiceMarket NewsGrains extend July hot streak on weather, war worriesJul 23, 2026|6 Min ReadNutrition & HealthFFAR project to strengthen U.S. screwworm preparednessJul 23, 2026|2 Min ReadAgribusiness NewsCooperative Ventures invests in OpenTug to advance ag logisticsJul 23, 2026|3 Min ReadNutrition & HealthNew compound shows promise for cattle methane reductionJul 23, 2026|2 Min ReadGoogle Preferred SourceSet Feedstuffs as a "preferred source" to get quicker access to the news you value.Add Us NowCurrent Conditions forNew York, NYChange Location83°FSunnyDay 85oNight 67o5.73 mph See Detailed Weather Reportchevron_rightcloseChange LocationEnter a zip code to see the weather conditions for a different location.Farm Progress ShowFarm Progress Show annually hosts more than 600 exhibitors displaying new farm equipment, tractors, combines and farm implements; seed and crop protection products; and many additional farm supplies and services.Learn MoreIngredient Market PricesdocsContent SpotlightFeedstuffs Ingredient Prices: July 2026The exclusive Feedstuffs feed ingredient price listings are now available for July 2026.Read More

Time
Publication date: 2026-07-22. Event date: Not explicitly stated, but research announcement is current.
Location and population
United States poultry industry. Research institutions: Iowa State University, Commonwealth Scientific and Industrial Research Organization (CSIRO). Funding: Foundation for Food and Agriculture Research (FFAR), U.S. Department of Agriculture.
Event
A $300,000 research grant funded by FFAR, Iowa State University, and CSIRO to identify genetic factors in birds that influence immune response to H5N1 and H7N9 avian influenza viruses. The project aims to find genetic targets for new therapies and develop poultry with greater natural resistance.
Scale, impact and risk
Highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) continues to threaten the U.S. poultry industry, causing billions of dollars in economic losses, disrupting food production, and increasing costs for farmers and consumers. The research addresses the need for new approaches beyond biosecurity measures.
Response, status and uncertainty
The research is in progress, using advanced genomic tools and novel laboratory models to understand bird responses to H5N1 and H7N9 infections. It supports the USDA's research priority on increasing farmer profitability and aims to provide alternative tools for protecting poultry flocks in the near and long term. No current outbreak or immediate response is described; the focus is on future prevention strategies.

CDC won’t publish report showing COVID-19 vaccine effectiveness; former pharmacy president sentenced in $33M compounded drug kickback scheme; mRNA bird flu vaccine hits late-stage testing – Morning Medical Update - Medical Economics

CDC won’t publish report showing COVID-19 vaccine effectiveness; former pharmacy president sentenced in $33M compounded drug kickback scheme; mRNA bird flu vaccine hits late-stage testing – Morning Medical Update - Medical Economics
新闻简报Moderna has launched a late-stage trial for mRNA-1018, the first mRNA-based pandemic bird flu vaccine, enrolling 4,000 healthy adults in the U.S. and U.K. Backed by a $54 million CEPI investment, the trial represents a significant advancement in pandemic preparedness. If approved, Moderna will commit 20% of manufacturing capacity to low- and middle-income countries at affordable prices during a declared pandemic. The vaccine's development leverages data from Moderna's seasonal flu candidate, mRNA-1010, currently under review in multiple countries. This milestone underscores the growing role of mRNA technology in addressing emerging infectious disease threats.

发布于 2026-07-22。证据中未指定事件日期。

美国和英国。健康成年人参加了 mRNA-1018 禽流感 疫苗 试验。

规模、影响与风险
The trial is backed by a $54 million investment from CEPI. If approved and a flu pandemic is declared, Moderna will reserve 20% of its manufacturing capacity for supply to low- and middle-income countries at affordable prices.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

CDC won’t publish report showing COVID-19 vaccine effectiveness; former pharmacy president sentenced in $33M compounded drug kickback scheme; mRNA bird flu vaccine hits late-stage testing – Morning Medical Update - Medical Economics

News Brief
Moderna has launched a late-stage trial for mRNA-1018, the first mRNA-based pandemic bird flu vaccine, enrolling 4,000 healthy adults in the U.S. and U.K. Backed by a $54 million CEPI investment, the trial represents a significant advancement in pandemic preparedness. If approved, Moderna will commit 20% of manufacturing capacity to low- and middle-income countries at affordable prices during a declared pandemic. The vaccine's development leverages data from Moderna's seasonal flu candidate, mRNA-1010, currently under review in multiple countries. This milestone underscores the growing role of mRNA technology in addressing emerging infectious disease threats.
Original source text (English)
The top news stories in medicine today. HHS cited concerns about the study’s methodology; the report found vaccines cut ED visits and hospitalizations by approximately half this past winter. A report showing the effectiveness of COVID-19 vaccines has been blocked from publication in the CDC's Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report (MMWR). "The manuscript was not accepted for publication" due to concerns about the "methodological approach estimating vaccine effectiveness," said HHS spokesman Andrew Nixon. The MMWR is one of the CDC's primary vehicles for publishing public health findings and is closely watched by physicians, researchers and state health officials. The decision comes under the leadership of HHS Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr., whose vaccine advisory panel last September scrapped a broad recommendation for COVID shots — a move since temporarily blocked by a federal court. Had it been published, the report would have shown that the vaccines reduced emergency department (ED) visits and hospitalizations among healthy adults by approximately ‌half this ⁠past winter, as reported by Reuters . Adam Brosius was ordered to pay $33 million in restitution and forfeit $27 million. A former New Jersey pharmacy president has been sentenced to 24 months in prison for his role in a $33 million health care fraud and kickback scheme involving medically unnecessary compounded medications including scar creams, pain creams, migraine medication and vitamins. Adam Brosius, 61, of Delray Beach, Florida, served as director of business development and later president of Main Avenue Pharmacy, a mail-order pharmacy in Clifton, New Jersey, which from 2014 through 2016 distributed pre-printed prescription pads featuring high-reimbursement compounded drug formulas to marketers nationwide. Those marketers passed the pads to telemedicine companies and physicians under financial arrangements, and Main Avenue paid kickbacks based on prescription volume and reimbursement amounts after billing Medicare, TRICARE and commercial insurers. The pharmacy collected approximately $33 million in reimbursements for compounded medications, more than $5.8 million of which came from TRICARE. In addition to his prison term, Brosius was ordered to pay $33 million in restitution and forfeit $27 million. The mRNA-1018 vaccine is the first pandemic bird flu shot made with mRNA technology to reach late-stage trials. Moderna has begun a late-stage trial of its experimental bird flu vaccine, mRNA-1018, enrolling approximately 4,000 healthy adults in the United States and United Kingdom — the first time an mRNA-based pandemic bird flu vaccine has reached this stage of testing. The trial is backed by a $54 million investment from the Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI), which stepped in after HHS Kennedy canceled more than $700 million in U.S. government contracts with Moderna for bird flu vaccine development last May. If approved and a flu pandemic is declared, Moderna said it would reserve 20% of its manufacturing capacity for supply to low- and middle-income countries at affordable prices. The company said any regulatory filing for mRNA-1018 would also be supported by data from its seasonal flu vaccine candidate, mRNA-1010, currently under review in the U.S., European Union, Canada and Australia. Nihar R. Desai, MD, MPH; Martha Gulati, MD, MS, FACC, FAHA, MASPC, FESC, FSCCT (hon), FRCP Edin

Moderna has initiated a late-stage trial of its experimental mRNA-1018 bird flu vaccine, enrolling approximately 4,000 healthy adults. This marks the first time an mRNA-based pandemic bird flu vaccine has reached this stage of testing.
Scale, impact and risk
The trial is backed by a $54 million investment from CEPI. If approved and a flu pandemic is declared, Moderna will reserve 20% of its manufacturing capacity for supply to low- and middle-income countries at affordable prices.
Response, status and uncertainty
The trial is currently underway. The mRNA-1018 vaccine is supported by data from Moderna's seasonal flu vaccine candidate, mRNA-1010, which is under review in the U.S., EU, Canada, and Australia. No unresolved information or limitations are reported in the supplied evidence.

检测到 H5N1 后禽流感报告激增 - RNZ

Bird flu reports surge after H5N1 detection - RNZ
新闻简报新西兰第一产业部 (MPI) 报告称,在两只 野生鸟类 中检测到 H5N1 病毒株后,禽流感通报激增。仅周六,MPI 就收到了 157 份鸟类死亡或不适的报告。首席兽医官 Mary van Andel 证实了 H5N1 病毒的检测,但强调尚未确认新的人类病例。报告的激增凸显出公众在初次发现后提高了警惕。MPI 继续监测情况并调查感染源。随着调查的进展,敦促公众向当局报告任何异常鸟类 死亡率。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-19。事件日期:2026年7月20日(星期六)。
地点与对象
受影响人群:野生鸟类。报告实体:第一产业部(MPI)。
事件
两个 野生鸟类 检测到 H5N1 后,禽流感报告激增。状态:确认在野生鸟类中检测到H5N1;没有新的人类确诊病例。
规模、影响与风险
仅周六就报告了 157 起鸟类死亡或不适的通知。没有新确诊的人类 H5N1 病例。风险:证据中未明确说明。
应对、状态与不确定性
MPI 正在调查并接收报告。首席兽医官玛丽·范安德尔提供了信息。没有新的人类确诊病例报告。未解决:有关调查状况和预防措施的详细信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu reports surge after H5N1 detection - RNZ

News Brief
New Zealand's Ministry of Primary Industries (MPI) reported a surge in bird flu notifications after detecting the H5N1 strain in two wild birds. On Saturday alone, MPI received 157 reports of dead or unwell birds. Chief veterinary officer Mary van Andel confirmed the H5N1 detection but emphasized no new human cases have been confirmed. The surge in reports highlights increased public vigilance following the initial detection. MPI continues to monitor the situation and investigate the source of the infections. The public is urged to report any unusual bird mortality to authorities as the investigation progresses.
Original source text (English)

Affected population: Wild birds. Reporting entity: Ministry of Primary Industries (MPI).
Event
Surge in bird flu reports after H5N1 detection in two wild birds. Status: Confirmed H5N1 detection in wild birds; no new confirmed human cases.
Scale, impact and risk
157 notifications of dead or unwell birds reported on Saturday alone. No new confirmed human cases of H5N1. Risk: Not explicitly stated in the evidence.
Response, status and uncertainty
MPI is investigating and receiving reports. Chief veterinary officer Mary van Andel provided information. No new confirmed human cases reported. Unresolved: Details on investigation status and preventive measures.

禽流感警告预防世界大流行 - 亚洲新闻

Bird flu warning prevents world pandemic - AsiaNews
新闻简报一名联合国官员证实,2006 年的早期预警和政府反应阻止了 H5N1 禽流感(禽流感)的直接流行。自 2003 年以来,已有 30 多个国家报告了 暴发,其中有 256 例人类病例,151 例死亡,主要发生在印度尼西亚。该病毒已感染全球(美洲除外)的鸟类,导致数百万只家禽被扑杀。尽管预防了直接的大流行,但联合国警告说,H5N1 至少在 5 至 10 年内仍然是全球主要威胁,需要持续的经济和组织援助,特别是对印度尼西亚和非洲。控制暴发的关键是改变家禽饲养方式,专家认为这需要5-10年才能有效实施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-21。事件日期:2006年10月25日(暴发报告),2月(发出警告)。
地点与对象
全球(美洲除外),有 30 多个国家报告 暴发。受影响最严重的国家:印度尼西亚。受影响人群:人类(2003年以来256例病例,151例死亡)和鸟类(扑杀数百万只家禽、鹅等)。
事件
在多个国家确认了 H5N1 禽流感(禽流感)的 暴发。联合国官员戴维·纳巴罗 (David Nabarro) 报告说,早期预警和政府反应阻止了 2006 年的一场大流行。
规模、影响与风险
自 2003 年以来,已有 256 例人类病例,151 人死亡。人类死亡人数每月呈上升趋势,尤其是在印度尼西亚。该病毒已感染全球(美洲除外)的鸟类,导致数百万只家禽被扑杀。风险:联合国认为 H5N1 是 5-10 年内的主要问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
联合国对政府在 2006 年预防大流行病的迅速反应感到满意。纳巴罗呼吁加强对印度尼西亚和非洲的经济和组织援助。主要应对措施:改变家禽饲养方式(预计需要 5-10 年)。病毒通过 野生鸟类 传播并进行受感染鸟类的贸易。来源限制:已审查完整的原始文章正文。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu warning prevents world pandemic - AsiaNews

News Brief
A UN official has confirmed that early warnings and government responses in 2006 prevented an immediate pandemic of H5N1 avian influenza (bird flu). More than 30 countries reported outbreaks, with 256 human cases and 151 deaths since 2003, primarily in Indonesia. The virus has infected birds globally (except the Americas), leading to the culling of millions of poultry. Despite the prevention of an immediate pandemic, the UN warns that H5N1 remains a major global threat for at least 5-10 years, requiring sustained economic and organizational assistance, particularly for Indonesia and Africa. Key to controlling the outbreak is changing poultry rearing practices, which experts believe will take 5-10 years to implement effectively.
Original source text (English)
Bird flu warning prevents world pandemic UN official says early warning on possible virus mutation generated right response in governments, preventing an immediate pandemic. He makes an appeal for Indonesia and Africa. October 25, 2006 New York (AsiaNews/Agencies)  More than 30 countries have recorded bird flu outbreaks. Although the number of H5N1 virus-related deaths is rising, the United Nations is satisfied that a quick response by governments last year prevented a pandemic, said David Nabarro, a senior UN health coordinator. In February Mr Nabarro warned countries against the possibility of person-to-person transmission or an imminent pandemic because of a possible mutation in the H5N1 virus. Except for the Americas, the virus has infected birds around the world and led to the cullying of millions of poultry, geese and other birds. "In 2006, we did see more than 30 countries reporting outbreaks," Nabarro said. "Unfortunately, the virus continues to affect humanswith 256 people known to be affected and 151 dying" since 2003. The monthly trend in bird flu-related human deaths is upward, especially because of Indonesia, the most affected country. The UN official has called for stepped up economic and organisational assistance, especially for Indonesia and Africa. In his view, the H5N1 avian influenza virus should remain a major issue for most of the world for at least five years, and perhaps 10 years. One key to tackling the bird flu is to change poultry rearing practices. Bird flu seems to be spread by a combination of wild birds that migrate and trade in infected birds. Many experts also believe it will take five to 10 years to change poultry rearing practices, especially in countries where poultry is plentiful and birds are kept in the backyard
View five news elements
Time
Publication date: 2026-07-21. Event date: October 25, 2006 (outbreaks reported), February (warning issued).
Location and population
Global (except Americas), with 30+ countries reporting outbreaks. Most affected country: Indonesia. Affected population: Humans (256 cases, 151 deaths since 2003) and birds (culled millions of poultry, geese, etc.).
Event
Confirmed outbreak of H5N1 avian influenza (bird flu) in multiple countries. UN official David Nabarro reported that early warnings and government responses prevented an immediate pandemic in 2006.
Scale, impact and risk
256 human cases, 151 deaths since 2003. Monthly trend in human deaths is upward, especially in Indonesia. Virus has infected birds globally (except Americas), leading to culling of millions of poultry. Risk: UN considers H5N1 a major issue for 5-10 years.
Response, status and uncertainty
UN satisfied with quick government response preventing pandemic in 2006. Nabarro called for stepped-up economic and organizational assistance for Indonesia and Africa. Key response: changing poultry rearing practices (expected to take 5-10 years). Virus spread via wild birds and trade in infected birds. Source limitation: Full original article body was reviewed.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Cook Islands on alert after H5N1 bird flu detected in New Zealand - Cook Islands News

News Brief
The Cook Islands Ministry of Agriculture has activated a precautionary alert following the detection of H5N1 bird flu in New Zealand. While no cases have been confirmed in the Cook Islands, the ministry is monitoring the situation closely and may restrict poultry imports from New Zealand if the virus is found in domestic flocks. The risk to the Cook Islands is currently assessed as low. This proactive measure aims to safeguard the local poultry industry and prevent potential outbreaks.
Original source text (English)

Brazil's Rio Grande do Sul Confirms Avian Influenza Case in Backyard Flock - foodmarket.com

News Brief
Brazil's Rio Grande do Sul state has confirmed its first case of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) in a backyard poultry flock in the municipality of Coqueiros do Sul. The diagnosis was officially confirmed by the Federal Agricultural Defense Laboratory in Campinas on July 17, 2026, following initial notification on July 14. This marks the state's first registered case of HPAI, prompting veterinary authorities to investigate and respond to the outbreak. The affected flock is located in the northwest region of Rio Grande do Sul, and authorities are monitoring the situation to prevent further spread of the virus.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

EU says Turkey's bird flu is lethal H5N1 strain - AsiaNews

News Brief
The European Commission has confirmed that the bird flu virus spreading in Turkey is the lethal H5N1 strain, following laboratory testing in Weybridge. The outbreak has resulted in at least three deaths in a single family near Van, with additional cases reported in Sanliurfa. Authorities have isolated affected families and placed over 20 people under observation nationwide. In response, the EU has maintained a ban on imports of live birds and poultry products from Turkey. The outbreak appears linked to consumption of infected poultry, with poultry merchants among the affected groups. Health authorities continue to monitor the situation as the number of suspected cases grows.
Original source text (English)
EU says Turkey's bird flu is lethal H5N1 strain The number of deaths and sick people is on the rise across Turkey. Turkey January 7, 2006 Brussels (AsiaNews/ Agencies)  The bird flu virus which has reached Turkey is the lethal H5N1 strain: this was confirmed by the European Commission after laboratory testing in Weybridge. In a statement released today, it was noted that an EU ban on imports from Turkey of live birds and poultry products would remain. Meanwhile the number of deaths and sick people in the country is growing. A third child, Hulya Kocyigit, 11 years, from the family which already lost two children to bird flu, died yesterday. Her brother Mehemet Ali, 14 years died last week and her sister Fatima, aged 15 years, died on 14 January. The family lives near Van, nearly on the eastern border with Iran. A family of Sanliurfa, on the southern border with Syria was admitted to hospital and placed in isolation after they presented symptoms of bird flu. Ibrahim Sakat, his wife and two children felt ill after eating an infected chicken. Sakat is a poultry merchant. Doctors have at least 20 people under observation across all the territory of Turkey, most of them youth suspected to have contracted the lethal virus

Cambridgeshire vet recognised for leadership in avian influenza response and data-driven welfare - VetSurgeon
新闻简报杰西卡·西尔 (Jessica Seale) 是剑桥郡 Slate Hall 兽医服务中心的高级助理,因其在 禽流感 应对措施和创新的数据驱动福利计划方面的卓越领导力而被授予 Zoetis 赞助的“年度农场兽医”称号。Seale 获得了伦敦国家蛋类和家禽奖的认可,已进行了 400 多次禽流感许可访问,并为英国首次 禽流感 疫苗接种 试验做出了贡献。她的工作包括开发自动化数据分析工具,将数千个数据点转化为兽医团队和生产者的实时健康见解。她是福利主导型生产的坚定倡导者,倡导肉鸡抗炎 治疗,并积极培训兽医学生和专业人员,同时支持抗生素监测计划。通过将科学、创新和合作融为一体,Seale 继续对鸡群健康、福利和英国家禽业的未来产生持久影响。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Cambridgeshire vet recognised for leadership in avian influenza response and data-driven welfare - VetSurgeon

News Brief
Jessica Seale, a senior associate at Slate Hall Veterinary Services in Cambridgeshire, has been honored as the Zoetis-sponsored 'Farm Vet of the Year' for her exceptional leadership in avian influenza response and innovative data-driven welfare initiatives. Recognized at the National Egg & Poultry Awards in London, Seale has conducted over 400 bird flu licensing visits and contributed to the UK's first avian influenza vaccination trials. Her work includes developing automated data analysis tools that transform thousands of data points into real-time health insights for veterinary teams and producers. A strong advocate for welfare-led production, she champions anti-inflammatory treatments in broilers and actively trains veterinary students and professionals while supporting antibiotic monitoring programmes. Through her blend of science, innovation, and collaboration, Seale continues to have a lasting impact on flock health, welfare, and the future of the UK poultry industry.
Original source text (English)
